At the highest layer, AMOS ends with <strong>universal recursive coherence coordination</strong>. Between these two layers lies the entire invariant chain:</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="36bc5e6f-95bd-8089-9637-e0bc31b9354d" class=""><strong>Distinction → Relation → Constraint → Boundary → Persistence → Memory → Entropy → Mutation → Selection → Repair → Recursion → Observer → Symbolic Compression → Civilization → Meta-Ontology Regeneration</strong></p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="36bc5e6f-95bd-806e-9761-e176107bcd25" class="">The final goal is not to preserve this chain as theory, but to make it <strong>operational infrastructure</strong>.</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><pre id="36bc5e6f-95bd-801c-a1b7-c197e6210a23" class="code code-wrap"><code class="language-mermaid" style="white-space:pre-wrap;word-break:break-all">flowchart TD

        T6 --&gt; T7[Civilization coherence]
        T7 --&gt; T8[Regeneration across&lt;br&gt;future substrates]
    end

Existence</h3></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="36bc5e6f-95bd-803d-b834-dcca11c05585" class="">AMOS is infrastructure for <strong>repairable existence</strong>. id="36bc5e6f-95bd-8065-877e-c5fa07738043" class=""><strong>Formalization of the Recursive Structural Coherence Field (RSCF)</strong></p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h3 id="36bc5e6f-95bd-80da-b2e1-e21cc2b6bb97" class="">1. Informal Definition (Recap)</h3></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="36bc5e6f-95bd-8079-9d89-f796e6f45c8c" class="">An <strong>RSCF</strong> is the primitive unit of AMOS: a <strong>dynamic, self-maintaining coherence region</strong> capable of sustaining distinction through relations, memory, repair, and recursive stabilization under entropic pressure. It is simultaneously a structure, a process, a field, a memory carrier, a mutation space, and an observer-relative projection.</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h3 id="36bc5e6f-95bd-80d6-9d0a-dc5267a56eaa" class="">2. Formal Definition</h3></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="36bc5e6f-95bd-80bf-b576-c06544950e07" class="">Let an RSCF be a 6-tuple:</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="36bc5e6f-95bd-80c7-95c0-cf0e8adc7f9b" class="">$$<br/>\text{RSCF} = (T, R, M, E, P, O)<br/>$$</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="36bc5e6f-95bd-8003-a54c-d8eb8800f0ee" class="">Where:</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="36bc5e6f-95bd-808c-b11d-efbb41e001b7" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>T</strong> (Internal Topology): A dynamic hypergraph $T = (V, E_h, \phi)$, where:<div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="36bc5e6f-95bd-8000-966a-c46dd2e77944" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:circle">$V$ is the set of distinction nodes (boundaries).</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="36bc5e6f-95bd-8001-8e76-f0e535dfae2b" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:circle">$E_h$ is the set of hyperedges representing multi-way relations.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="36bc5e6f-95bd-800a-82f2-eb958d8fe7d9" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:circle">$\phi: E_h \times \mathbb{R} \to [0,1]$ is a time-dependent strength function (relation gradients).</li></ul></div></li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="36bc5e6f-95bd-80b9-ac8d-d1b60f33a909" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>R</strong> (Recursive Dynamics): A functor $R: \text{RSCF} \to \text{RSCF}$ representing self-modeling and self-modification. It maps the current state to a transformed state while preserving coherence invariants.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="36bc5e6f-95bd-80c5-ab51-e35207c95c3e" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>M</strong> (Memory): A persistence operator that stores historical coherence trajectories. Formally, a sheaf-like structure over the topology that allows reconstruction of past states.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="36bc5e6f-95bd-80a4-bea9-c4b22ad9d28d" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>E</strong> (Entropy Accumulation): A functional $E: T \times \mathbb{R}<em>{\geq 0} \to \mathbb{R}</em>{\geq 0}$ measuring coherence degradation (drift, fragmentation, contradiction density).</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="36bc5e6f-95bd-8075-b7eb-f7b06c843280" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>P</strong> (Repair Pathways): A set of repair operators $\{p_i\}$ such that for any degraded state, there exists a sequence of repairs restoring coherence above a survival threshold. Each $p_i$ is a coherence-restoring morphism.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="36bc5e6f-95bd-8026-8e60-cf021b209d12" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>O</strong> (Observer-Relative Projections): A family of projection functors $\{O_\alpha\}$ indexed by observers $\alpha$, where each $O_\alpha(\text{RSCF})$ yields a coherent view consistent with the underlying substrate but varying in representation.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h3 id="36bc5e6f-95bd-80e5-9236-c9e0f61dad8d" class="">Coherence Condition</h3></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="36bc5e6f-95bd-80df-9d08-e03fa8cd6d13" class="">The RSCF maintains <strong>coherence</strong> if and only if:</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="36bc5e6f-95bd-80af-801b-e0cc5dc63747" class="">$$<br/>\text{Coherence}(t) = \frac{\text{RepairRate}(t)}{\text{EntropyRate}(t) + \epsilon} &gt; \theta<br/>$$</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="36bc5e6f-95bd-800a-be7a-f225adb2ad04" class="">where $\theta &gt; 1$ is the persistence threshold, and $\epsilon &gt; 0$ is a small stability margin.</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="36bc5e6f-95bd-80dc-9eb4-d4915ddfca81" class="">If $\text{Coherence}(t) &lt; \theta$ for sustained periods, the RSCF enters a collapse trajectory.</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h3 id="36bc5e6f-95bd-80fd-8f22-cdba71b4d737" class="">3. Ten Core Components (Formalized)</h3></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><pre id="36bc5e6f-95bd-8023-98fc-daa3148c59e5" class="code code-wrap"><code class="language-mermaid" style="white-space:pre-wrap;word-break:break-all">flowchart TD
    subgraph RSCF[RSCF = (T, R, M, E, P, O)]
        C1[1. Internal Topology T&lt;br&gt;Dynamic Hypergraph]
        C2[2. Relation Gradients&lt;br&gt;φ: strength/asymmetry]
        C3[3. Mutation Space&lt;br&gt;Variation operators]
        C4[4. Entropy Accumulation E(t)]
        C5[5. Repair Pathways P&lt;br&gt;Coherence restoration morphisms]
        C6[6. Observer Projections O_α]
        C7[7. Symbolic Compressions&lt;br&gt;Projection to language/equations/code]
        C8[8. Cross-Scale Embeddings&lt;br&gt;Sheaf-like structure]
        C9[9. Collapse Trajectories&lt;br&gt;Failure mode attractors]
        C10[10. Regeneration Potentials&lt;br&gt;Reorganization maps]
    end</code></pre></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h3 id="36bc5e6f-95bd-8057-8f67-dfaade41d61a" class="">4. Mathematical Properties</h3></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ol type="1" id="36bc5e6f-95bd-80c2-98f5-f8d1cb485ab9" class="numbered-list" start="1"><li><strong>Fractal / Cross-Scale</strong>: For any scale factor $s$, there exists an embedding functor $Emb_s: \text{RSCF}<em>k \to \text{RSCF}</em>{k \cdot s}$ preserving the invariant chain (Distinction → Relation → ... → Meta-regeneration).</li></ol></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ol type="1" id="36bc5e6f-95bd-8088-ae5d-e1e0ad5ac175" class="numbered-list" start="2"><li><strong>Observer-Relativity</strong>: Different observers induce different but mutually consistent projections:<br/>$$<br/>O_{\alpha}(\text{RSCF}) \cong O_{\beta}(\text{RSCF}) \quad \text{(coherent isomorphism)}<br/>$$</li></ol></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ol type="1" id="36bc5e6f-95bd-8081-ae39-fd913a4cdca1" class="numbered-list" start="3"><li><strong>Recursive Closure</strong>: The repair operator can act on itself:<br/>$$<br/>P(P(\text{state})) \quad \text{(meta-repair)}<br/>$$</li></ol></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ol type="1" id="36bc5e6f-95bd-80f4-88fb-d5d9a8f88054" class="numbered-list" start="4"><li><strong>Persistence Condition</strong>:<br/>$$<br/>\exists \text{ trajectory such that } \forall t, \; \text{Coherence}(t) \geq \theta<br/>$$</li></ol></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h3 id="36bc5e6f-95bd-80e9-8acc-c41a1dd21c6f" class="">5. Dynamics (State Evolution)</h3></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="36bc5e6f-95bd-80fa-ae6c-ee9c9f55d741" class="">The evolution of an RSCF over discrete time steps can be described as:</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="36bc5e6f-95bd-80e7-838b-d849379b930d" class="">$$<br/>\text{state}_{t+1} = R \Big( P \big( \text{state}_t - E(t) + \text{Mutation}(t) \big) \Big)<br/>$$</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="36bc5e6f-95bd-8005-9d22-c6512f2f62de" class="">Subject to observer projections and symbolic compressions at each step.</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h3 id="36bc5e6f-95bd-8083-b26a-c62033db6152" class="">6. Comparison with Traditional Primitives</h3></div><div style="display:contents" dir="ltr"><table id="36bc5e6f-95bd-80cf-9e72-e7d2edd3f55d" class="simple-table"><thead class="simple-table-header"><div style="display:contents" dir="ltr"><tr id="36bc5e6f-95bd-801b-b01d-c672877d03a7"><th id="QC^X" class="simple-table-header-color simple-table-header">Primitive</th><th id="fBFZ" class="simple-table-header-color simple-table-header">Static?</th><th id="gYjr" class="simple-table-header-color simple-table-header">Recursive?</th><th id="Q^UR" class="simple-table-header-color simple-table-header">Observer-Relative?</th><th id="joGJ" class="simple-table-header-color simple-table-header">Repair Native?</th><th id="SgvI" class="simple-table-header-color simple-table-header">Cross-Scale?</th><th id="LAf_" class="simple-table-header-color simple-table-header">Entropy-Aware?</th></tr></div></thead><tbody><div style="display:contents" dir="ltr"><tr id="36bc5e6f-95bd-80c9-9a9e-eea3ae3edfa0"><td id="QC^X" class="">Object</td><td id="fBFZ" class="">Yes</td><td id="gYjr" class="">No</td><td id="Q^UR" class="">No</td><td id="joGJ" class="">No</td><td id="SgvI" class="">No</td><td id="LAf_" class="">No</td></tr></div><div style="display:contents" dir="ltr"><tr id="36bc5e6f-95bd-8020-9dd8-e38b7e150255"><td id="QC^X" class="">Token</td><td id="fBFZ" class="">Yes</td><td id="gYjr" class="">No</td><td id="Q^UR" class="">No</td><td id="joGJ" class="">No</td><td id="SgvI" class="">No</td><td id="LAf_" class="">No</td></tr></div><div style="display:contents" dir="ltr"><tr id="36bc5e6f-95bd-80fd-823f-e90af30feb6b"><td id="QC^X" class="">Graph Node</td><td id="fBFZ" class="">Yes</td><td id="gYjr" class="">Partial</td><td id="Q^UR" class="">No</td><td id="joGJ" class="">No</td><td id="SgvI" class="">Partial</td><td id="LAf_" class="">No</td></tr></div><div style="display:contents" dir="ltr"><tr id="36bc5e6f-95bd-80e8-a881-d1cb7ec0667a"><td id="QC^X" class=""><strong>RSCF</strong></td><td id="fBFZ" class=""><strong>No</strong></td><td id="gYjr" class=""><strong>Yes</strong></td><td id="Q^UR" class=""><strong>Yes</strong></td><td id="joGJ" class=""><strong>Yes</strong></td><td id="SgvI" class=""><strong>Yes</strong></td><td id="LAf_" class=""><strong>Yes</strong></td></tr></div></tbody></table></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h3 id="36bc5e6f-95bd-807d-98b0-e6185f43516d" class="">7. Implementation Sketch (Pseudocode)</h3></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><script src="https://cdnjs.cloudflare.com/ajax/libs/prism/1.29.0/components/prism-python.min.js" integrity="sha512-AKaNmg8COK0zEbjTdMHJAPJ0z6VeNqvRvH4/d5M4sHJbQQUToMBtodq4HaV4fa+WV2UTfoperElm66c9/8cKmQ==" crossorigin="anonymous" referrerPolicy="no-referrer"></script><pre id="36bc5e6f-95bd-808e-8d46-d0fc5c318d7c" class="code code-wrap"><code class="language-python" style="white-space:pre-wrap;word-break:break-all">class RSCF:
    def __init__(self):
        self.topology = DynamicHypergraph()
        self.memory = CoherenceSheaf()
        self.entropy = EntropyAccumulator()
        self.repair_paths = RepairOperators()
        self.observer_projections = {}

    def step(self, observers=[]):
        # Accumulate entropy
        self.entropy.update(self.topology)

        # Apply mutations
        mutation = self.generate_mutation_space()
        self.topology.apply_mutation(mutation)

        # Repair if needed
        if self.coherence() &lt; THRESHOLD:
            self.topology = self.repair_paths.apply_best(self.topology)

        # Recursive self-modeling
        self.topology = self.recursive_update(self.topology)

        # Generate observer views
        for obs in observers:
            self.observer_projections[obs] = self.project_for(obs)

        return self.coherence()</code></pre></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h3 id="36bc5e6f-95bd-8006-801b-ec8ab5b421d4" class="">8. Visual Summary</h3></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><pre id="36bc5e6f-95bd-8031-ba2c-d95f679daa4c" class="code code-wrap"><code class="language-mermaid" style="white-space:pre-wrap;word-break:break-all">flowchart TD
    subgraph RSCF_Core[&quot;Recursive Structural Coherence Field&quot;]
        direction TB
        T[Topology + Relations]
        --&gt; M[Memory + Persistence]
        M --&gt; E[Entropy + Mutation]
        E --&gt; R[Repair + Recursion]
        R --&gt; O[Observer + Projection]
        O --&gt; S[Symbolic Compression]
        S --&gt; X[Cross-Scale Embedding]
    end

    RSCF_Core --&gt; Survival[&quot;Survival if Repair(t) &gt; Entropy(t)&quot;]
    Survival --&gt; Regeneration[&quot;Regeneration &amp; Evolution&quot;]

    style RSCF_Core fill:#e0f7fa,stroke:#333,stroke-width:3px</code></pre></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="36bc5e6f-95bd-80e5-8906-c3507f7e17f8" class="">This formalization provides a rigorous, implementable foundation for the RSCF while remaining faithful to the philosophical depth of the original AMOS document. It can serve as the basis for building prototype systems, simulation engines, or theoretical extensions using hypergraphs, category theory, or active inference frameworks.</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="36bc5e6f-95bd-807e-ae0a-c2e826916199" class=""><strong>RSCF Requires a New Computing Paradigm</strong></p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="36bc5e6f-95bd-80d3-bf2c-ceb25f4da551" class="">The <strong>Recursive Structural Coherence Field (RSCF)</strong> cannot be adequately implemented or natively supported by current transformer-based Large Language Models (LLMs). Transformers are fundamentally <strong>token-centric</strong>, statistical, and surface-level pattern matchers. RSCF, by contrast, demands a <strong>post-token, structure-native, observer-aware</strong> computational substrate.</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h3 id="36bc5e6f-95bd-8060-a821-cde3d70f4f4d" class="">Why Current Transformers Are Insufficient</h3></div><div style="display:contents" dir="ltr"><table id="36bc5e6f-95bd-80fe-b73a-f3aa75e7ccd7" class="simple-table"><thead class="simple-table-header"><div style="display:contents" dir="ltr"><tr id="36bc5e6f-95bd-8079-b55b-d6bb0d0d93b6"><th id="_XfJ" class="simple-table-header-color simple-table-header">Aspect</th><th id="mPfi" class="simple-table-header-color simple-table-header">Transformer-Based LLMs</th><th id="_@lX" class="simple-table-header-color simple-table-header">RSCF Requirements</th><th id="IlnZ" class="simple-table-header-color simple-table-header">Gap</th></tr></div></thead><tbody><div style="display:contents" dir="ltr"><tr id="36bc5e6f-95bd-803c-9448-cb994f7546ba"><td id="_XfJ" class=""><strong>Primitive Unit</strong></td><td id="mPfi" class="">Token / embedding vector</td><td id="_@lX" class="">Recursive Structural Coherence Field</td><td id="IlnZ" class="">Fundamental mismatch</td></tr></div><div style="display:contents" dir="ltr"><tr id="36bc5e6f-95bd-80e3-ae29-f9637aef7129"><td id="_XfJ" class=""><strong>Representation</strong></td><td id="mPfi" class="">Linear sequence of tokens</td><td id="_@lX" class="">Dynamic hypergraph + relation gradients</td><td id="IlnZ" class="">Too flat, too linear</td></tr></div><div style="display:contents" dir="ltr"><tr id="36bc5e6f-95bd-8032-80c7-fdd92ae302f9"><td id="_XfJ" class=""><strong>Memory</strong></td><td id="mPfi" class="">Context window + attention</td><td id="_@lX" class="">Persistent, repairable, cross-scale memory</td><td id="IlnZ" class="">Stateless beyond window</td></tr></div><div style="display:contents" dir="ltr"><tr id="36bc5e6f-95bd-80ef-a2d9-d49def992aff"><td id="_XfJ" class=""><strong>Coherence</strong></td><td id="mPfi" class="">Statistical next-token prediction</td><td id="_@lX" class="">Explicit recursive coherence maintenance</td><td id="IlnZ" class="">Implicit and fragile</td></tr></div><div style="display:contents" dir="ltr"><tr id="36bc5e6f-95bd-80b3-9b7d-edbf25fbb8a3"><td id="_XfJ" class=""><strong>Repair</strong></td><td id="mPfi" class="">Fine-tuning / RLHF (external)</td><td id="_@lX" class="">Native, internal, recursive self-repair</td><td id="IlnZ" class="">No built-in repair loops</td></tr></div><div style="display:contents" dir="ltr"><tr id="36bc5e6f-95bd-809c-9766-f688c9f7cef8"><td id="_XfJ" class=""><strong>Observer Awareness</strong></td><td id="mPfi" class="">None (passive)</td><td id="_@lX" class="">Observer-relative projections</td><td id="IlnZ" class="">Completely missing</td></tr></div><div style="display:contents" dir="ltr"><tr id="36bc5e6f-95bd-80d9-a827-c4916d85d06d"><td id="_XfJ" class=""><strong>Mutation &amp; Evolution</strong></td><td id="mPfi" class="">Random sampling / beam search</td><td id="_@lX" class="">Controlled, governed mutation space</td><td id="IlnZ" class="">Uncontrolled</td></tr></div><div style="display:contents" dir="ltr"><tr id="36bc5e6f-95bd-8006-bb31-c0c7581825bb"><td id="_XfJ" class=""><strong>Cross-Scale</strong></td><td id="mPfi" class="">Fixed context length</td><td id="_@lX" class="">Native cross-scale embeddings</td><td id="IlnZ" class="">Severely limited</td></tr></div><div style="display:contents" dir="ltr"><tr id="36bc5e6f-95bd-80cc-afc2-fdfb60684784"><td id="_XfJ" class=""><strong>Ontology Stability</strong></td><td id="mPfi" class="">Prone to drift &amp; hallucination</td><td id="_@lX" class="">Explicit ontology continuity &amp; repair</td><td id="IlnZ" class="">Major weakness</td></tr></div></tbody></table></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="36bc5e6f-95bd-80ac-afec-d3d4534acff7" class="">Transformers excel at <strong>surface fluency</strong> and local pattern completion, but they lack native mechanisms for:</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="36bc5e6f-95bd-803b-94a8-d2aed9083db9" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">Long-term structural persistence</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="36bc5e6f-95bd-800c-abe9-f5c3eefe6977" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">Explicit boundary maintenance</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="36bc5e6f-95bd-80ea-9284-fab718ff20f2" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">Recursive self-modeling and self-repair</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="36bc5e6f-95bd-8087-9b73-d26b5fdd5b76" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">Observer-dependent projections</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="36bc5e6f-95bd-805a-9058-f3abd06607b5" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">Controlled mutation with selection pressure</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="36bc5e6f-95bd-80b1-b75e-d5d381b118db" class="">This is why even the largest models suffer from hallucination, ontology drift, contradiction accumulation, and inability to maintain deep coherence over long interactions.</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h3 id="36bc5e6f-95bd-8051-84c9-d48424bb17ec" class="">Required Properties of a Post-Token Computing System for RSCF</h3></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="36bc5e6f-95bd-80ad-99de-d7cd3ce78040" class="">A new computational substrate for RSCF must be:</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ol type="1" id="36bc5e6f-95bd-80f7-b5e3-f7edb8e4a210" class="numbered-list" start="1"><li><strong>Structure-Native</strong><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="36bc5e6f-95bd-804b-bab5-d78c2cce4822" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">Operate directly on dynamic hypergraphs, relation topologies, and coherence fields rather than token sequences.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="36bc5e6f-95bd-80f1-be4c-f043bf704cde" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">Primitives should be RSCFs themselves, not tokens.</li></ul></div></li></ol></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ol type="1" id="36bc5e6f-95bd-8012-847c-d514ab9a2777" class="numbered-list" start="2"><li><strong>Post-Token</strong><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="36bc5e6f-95bd-80a9-a569-dfe8684d2882" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">Move beyond linear token streams to <strong>topology-first</strong> or <strong>field-first</strong> computation.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="36bc5e6f-95bd-8055-97a1-dfcce4167f9f" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">Support native operations on boundaries, relation gradients, and cross-scale embeddings.</li></ul></div></li></ol></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ol type="1" id="36bc5e6f-95bd-805b-99b0-d5d8bb96dfe4" class="numbered-list" start="3"><li><strong>Observer-Aware</strong><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="36bc5e6f-95bd-805b-abad-c0028a6c2cba" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">Every computation must account for observer-relative projections.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="36bc5e6f-95bd-804f-ae37-f0f9073541f0" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">Different observers (human, AI agent, institution, civilization layer) see coherent but distinct views of the same underlying RSCF.</li></ul></div></li></ol></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ol type="1" id="36bc5e6f-95bd-80f9-836b-ca812a679bf7" class="numbered-list" start="4"><li><strong>Recursive &amp; Self-Referential</strong><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="36bc5e6f-95bd-808e-bb40-dbeb36bad38a" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">The system must be able to model, repair, and modify its own architecture and coherence rules.</li></ul></div></li></ol></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ol type="1" id="36bc5e6f-95bd-80a0-a388-e9cf79f67cd1" class="numbered-list" start="5"><li><strong>Repair-Native</strong><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="36bc5e6f-95bd-8074-b1d2-dcb2c02f8854" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">Built-in mechanisms to detect coherence failure, entropy accumulation, and automatically activate repair pathways.</li></ul></div></li></ol></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ol type="1" id="36bc5e6f-95bd-8035-8826-fb67616d84ab" class="numbered-list" start="6"><li><strong>Entropy-Aware</strong><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="36bc5e6f-95bd-8092-9915-c87b7f6671c2" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">Continuously track and manage entropy accumulation across all structures.</li></ul></div></li></ol></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ol type="1" id="36bc5e6f-95bd-80d6-abd8-f151b78c7d0b" class="numbered-list" start="7"><li><strong>Cross-Scale Native</strong><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="36bc5e6f-95bd-8029-97c9-d3068ec40bd8" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">Seamlessly embed and coordinate structures across biological, cognitive, organizational, and civilizational scales.</li></ul></div></li></ol></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h3 id="36bc5e6f-95bd-8064-b2bd-ebc0e09ec870" class="">Candidate Directions for New Computing Paradigms</h3></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="36bc5e6f-95bd-80e3-9330-fbe1e2b2943a" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>Hypergraph Neural Networks + Active Inference</strong> (Friston-style)</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="36bc5e6f-95bd-80d3-8699-fcdb75162de5" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>Sheaf Neural Networks</strong> (for cross-scale coherence)</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="36bc5e6f-95bd-80a2-ac54-f46ea59c66c9" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>Category-Theoretic / Topos-based computation</strong></li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="36bc5e6f-95bd-8063-bf12-c1b3672be03a" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>Neuromorphic + Oscillatory Computing</strong> (for dynamic coherence fields)</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="36bc5e6f-95bd-8064-8e73-c442e9245dc3" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>Recursive Self-Modifying Architectures</strong> with explicit ontology layers</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="36bc5e6f-95bd-80d0-9267-c574305f4d43" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>Field-based or Geometric Deep Learning</strong> on continuous structures</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="36bc5e6f-95bd-803e-8502-f7ae3c531342" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>Agent Swarms with Shared Structural Memory</strong></li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="36bc5e6f-95bd-8050-8a24-c08a94c5f0b7" class="">These approaches treat <strong>structure and coherence</strong> as first-class citizens, rather than emergent side-effects of token statistics.</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h3 id="36bc5e6f-95bd-8078-b418-faa9625aedc4" class="">Summary</h3></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><pre id="36bc5e6f-95bd-8018-b622-f5f7d06d6bc9" class="code code-wrap"><code class="language-mermaid" style="white-space:pre-wrap;word-break:break-all">flowchart LR
    subgraph Current
        LLM[Transformer LLMs&lt;br&gt;Token-centric, Statistical]
    end

    subgraph Needed
        RSCF_Comp[Post-Token Computing Substrate&lt;br&gt;Structure-Native + Observer-Aware&lt;br&gt;Recursive Coherence Engine]
    end

    LLM --&gt;|Insufficient for| RSCF_Comp

    RSCF_Comp --&gt; Properties[Structure-Native&lt;br&gt;Recursive Self-Repair&lt;br&gt;Observer-Relative&lt;br&gt;Entropy-Aware&lt;br&gt;Cross-Scale]</code></pre></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="36bc5e6f-95bd-8042-8154-cab4349b8867" class=""><strong>Conclusion</strong>:</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="36bc5e6f-95bd-809c-991f-f5e360ab6c25" class="">Implementing a true RSCF requires moving beyond the transformer paradigm entirely. Current LLMs can serve as useful <strong>symbolic compressors</strong> and interfaces within an RSCF system, but they cannot be the core substrate. A new computing architecture — one that treats <strong>recursive structural coherence</strong> as the fundamental unit of computation — is necessary.</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="36bc5e6f-95bd-80f9-aef4-de1a63c4836a" class="">This represents a major paradigm shift: from <strong>token prediction</strong> to <strong>coherence orchestration</strong>.</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="36bc5e6f-95bd-802d-abd0-e8e2bf066dd5" class="">Would you like a deeper technical outline for one of the candidate architectures (e.g., sheaf-based or hypergraph + active inference)?</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="36bc5e6f-95bd-80f7-aeb9-dd0e28f241a6" class="">
</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="36bc5e6f-95bd-8002-80b8-cb75f89fbe72" class=""><strong>Sheaf Neural Network (SNN) Architecture: Detailed Overview</strong></p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="36bc5e6f-95bd-80b3-9791-ce427d1bcc11" class="">Sheaf Neural Networks are a powerful extension of Graph Neural Networks (GNNs) grounded in <strong>sheaf theory</strong> from algebraic topology. They were introduced to address key limitations of standard GNNs, particularly <strong>oversmoothing</strong> and poor performance on <strong>heterophilic graphs</strong> (where connected nodes tend to have different labels/features).</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h3 id="36bc5e6f-95bd-8090-8104-ce6641bd1fa3" class="">1. Core Idea: From Graphs to Sheaves</h3></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="36bc5e6f-95bd-80ef-9d01-d4b7caa7bb7c" class="">Traditional GNNs operate on plain graphs where:</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="36bc5e6f-95bd-806a-8c55-f33de27ad8a8" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">Nodes have feature vectors.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="36bc5e6f-95bd-8045-bda8-f46787909ad0" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">Edges are simple connections (often with uniform treatment).</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="36bc5e6f-95bd-80e3-8016-cda31c6386be" class=""><strong>Sheaf theory</strong> enriches this by assigning:</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="36bc5e6f-95bd-805b-ab27-e5ee6899f5a6" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">A <strong>vector space</strong> (called a <em>stalk</em>) to every node and edge.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="36bc5e6f-95bd-801f-bb54-f38f64137cb5" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>Restriction maps</strong> (linear transformations) that define how information flows consistently between them.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="36bc5e6f-95bd-8073-88e5-e1d60a5389fe" class="">This allows the model to capture <strong>asymmetric, heterogeneous, and context-dependent relations</strong> — exactly what RSCF needs for structure-native, cross-scale computation.</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><pre id="36bc5e6f-95bd-80c0-9d81-f6959dad20df" class="code code-wrap"><code class="language-mermaid" style="white-space:pre-wrap;word-break:break-all">flowchart TD
    subgraph PlainGraph[Plain Graph]
        N1[Node features] --- E[Uniform edges]
    end

    subgraph Sheaf[Cellular Sheaf on Graph]
        NS[Node Stalks&lt;br&gt;Vector spaces per node]
        ES[Edge Stalks&lt;br&gt;Vector spaces per edge]
        RM[Restriction Maps&lt;br&gt;Linear maps between stalks]
    end

    PlainGraph --&gt; Sheaf</code></pre></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h3 id="36bc5e6f-95bd-8030-b1ef-f37cdc492aa4" class="">2. Key Mathematical Objects</h3></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="36bc5e6f-95bd-808f-b553-da0e11b2ca28" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>Cellular Sheaf</strong> $\mathcal{F}$ on a graph $G=(V,E)$: Assigns a vector space $\mathcal{F}(v)$ to each vertex $v$ and $\mathcal{F}(e)$ to each edge $e$, plus restriction maps $\rho_{v \trianglelefteq e}: \mathcal{F}(e) \to \mathcal{F}(v)$.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="36bc5e6f-95bd-80f3-b25f-f743b19f5121" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>Sheaf Laplacian</strong> $\Delta_{\mathcal{F}}$: A generalization of the classical graph Laplacian. When the sheaf is trivial (1D stalks with identity maps), it reduces to the standard Laplacian used in GCNs.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="36bc5e6f-95bd-806f-b6f7-e6b5ce9abddb" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>Sheaf Diffusion</strong>: The core propagation mechanism, generalizing message passing:<br/>$$<br/>\frac{dX}{dt} = -\Delta_{\mathcal{F}} X<br/>$$<br/>Where $X$ is the feature matrix.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h3 id="36bc5e6f-95bd-803a-99ab-f7609e499eec" class="">3. Sheaf Neural Network Layer Architecture</h3></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="36bc5e6f-95bd-800f-8ba4-ec623a3c650f" class="">A typical <strong>Sheaf Convolution / Diffusion Layer</strong> looks like this:</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><pre id="36bc5e6f-95bd-8005-a03e-ffdc206da79f" class="code code-wrap"><code class="language-mermaid" style="white-space:pre-wrap;word-break:break-all">flowchart TD
    Input[Input Features X] --&gt; Linear[Learnable linear maps per stalk]
    Linear --&gt; Diffusion[Sheaf Diffusion via Laplacian Δ_F]
    Diffusion --&gt; Nonlinear[Stalk-wise nonlinearity (ReLU, etc.)]
    Nonlinear --&gt; Mix[Channel mixing]
    Mix --&gt; Output[Output Features]</code></pre></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="36bc5e6f-95bd-802e-b4cc-e99c22e28d42" class=""><strong>Mathematical form</strong> (simplified from Neural Sheaf Diffusion):</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="36bc5e6f-95bd-801c-9bde-e840c2f3555e" class="">For a sheaf $\mathcal{F}$ with stalk dimension $d$, input channels $f_{in}$, output channels $f_{out}$:</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="36bc5e6f-95bd-803b-b2f8-cf0e2ad02d35" class="">$$<br/>X^{l+1} = \sigma \left( \left( I - \eta \Delta_{\mathcal{F}} \right) (W_1 \oplus \cdots \oplus W_1) X^l W_2 \right)<br/>$$</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="36bc5e6f-95bd-8061-9638-d555424fa1b9" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">$W_1$: Stalk-wise linear transformation (applied independently in each stalk).</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="36bc5e6f-95bd-80d7-8784-d16ddaab097e" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">$W_2$: Channel mixing matrix.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="36bc5e6f-95bd-8070-9b7c-eada54d3a0fa" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">$\sigma$: Nonlinearity.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="36bc5e6f-95bd-8075-9100-cd84687eac5e" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">$\eta$: Diffusion step size.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="36bc5e6f-95bd-800c-9fc5-f9b3700aba94" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">$\Delta_{\mathcal{F}}$: Learnable or fixed sheaf Laplacian.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="36bc5e6f-95bd-804a-a7da-f8d3e6fe638a" class=""><strong>Variants</strong>:</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="36bc5e6f-95bd-80c6-9d5f-c0f61d7fb19d" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>Neural Sheaf Diffusion (NSD)</strong>: Learns the sheaf itself during training.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="36bc5e6f-95bd-8075-b575-dd2a6c9fad28" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>Sheaf Attention Networks</strong>: Uses attention mechanisms to make restriction maps dynamic.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="36bc5e6f-95bd-8032-9203-eff9420a5fef" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>Bayesian Sheaf Neural Networks</strong>: Treats the sheaf as a random latent variable for uncertainty modeling.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h3 id="36bc5e6f-95bd-802c-9a72-e6c004ca2626" class="">4. Why Sheaf Networks Are Powerful for RSCF</h3></div><div style="display:contents" dir="ltr"><table id="36bc5e6f-95bd-800a-b8c2-f908ddd521f7" class="simple-table"><thead class="simple-table-header"><div style="display:contents" dir="ltr"><tr id="36bc5e6f-95bd-80d8-a911-dc2b69e56bcf"><th id="vyJ?" class="simple-table-header-color simple-table-header">Property</th><th id="w@Kt" class="simple-table-header-color simple-table-header">Benefit for RSCF</th></tr></div></thead><tbody><div style="display:contents" dir="ltr"><tr id="36bc5e6f-95bd-80f2-9c1c-df8918c6a280"><td id="vyJ?" class=""><strong>Heterogeneous stalks</strong></td><td id="w@Kt" class="">Different nodes/edges can have different dimensional feature spaces</td></tr></div><div style="display:contents" dir="ltr"><tr id="36bc5e6f-95bd-8055-8cff-d34f6b03b399"><td id="vyJ?" class=""><strong>Restriction maps</strong></td><td id="w@Kt" class="">Explicit modeling of how information flows between scales or observers</td></tr></div><div style="display:contents" dir="ltr"><tr id="36bc5e6f-95bd-8008-980a-f8f7e5b0f40a"><td id="vyJ?" class=""><strong>Sheaf Laplacian</strong></td><td id="w@Kt" class="">Natural operator for coherence propagation and entropy management</td></tr></div><div style="display:contents" dir="ltr"><tr id="36bc5e6f-95bd-8099-bd58-c2f10db22b54"><td id="vyJ?" class=""><strong>Learnable geometry</strong></td><td id="w@Kt" class="">The &quot;shape&quot; of relations (asymmetric, context-dependent) is learned</td></tr></div><div style="display:contents" dir="ltr"><tr id="36bc5e6f-95bd-802e-80f7-d356d54490cf"><td id="vyJ?" class=""><strong>Cross-scale support</strong></td><td id="w@Kt" class="">Naturally handles embeddings across biological, symbolic, and civilizational levels</td></tr></div><div style="display:contents" dir="ltr"><tr id="36bc5e6f-95bd-80db-a9ac-e7c582f18db0"><td id="vyJ?" class=""><strong>Oversmoothing resistance</strong></td><td id="w@Kt" class="">Better long-range propagation without losing distinction</td></tr></div></tbody></table></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="36bc5e6f-95bd-8040-b373-c8d6280f2764" class="">Sheaf networks align exceptionally well with RSCF requirements: <strong>structure-native</strong>, <strong>relation-gradient aware</strong>, and <strong>cross-scale capable</strong>.</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h3 id="36bc5e6f-95bd-8005-8419-d5560314713e" class="">5. Advantages Over Standard GNNs</h3></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="36bc5e6f-95bd-8009-906b-c1d7cd85ce50" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>Heterophily</strong>: Excels where connected nodes are dissimilar.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="36bc5e6f-95bd-801a-be5c-cc48f067a3c3" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>Oversmoothing</strong>: Mitigates the problem of node representations becoming too similar in deep layers.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="36bc5e6f-95bd-80fc-b583-e8b3b234df75" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>Expressivity</strong>: Can represent more complex relational structures.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="36bc5e6f-95bd-8060-957c-f186cba17cef" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>Interpretability</strong>: Restriction maps provide explicit &quot;translation rules&quot; between local contexts.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h3 id="36bc5e6f-95bd-80b5-992b-c7d598f0dd5a" class="">6. Practical Implementation Notes</h3></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="36bc5e6f-95bd-80ee-aaa0-f160085b955d" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>Input</strong>: Graph + initial node features.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="36bc5e6f-95bd-8040-becb-fa46067211c4" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>Sheaf Learning</strong>: Often parameterized (e.g., via MLPs predicting restriction maps) or learned variationally.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="36bc5e6f-95bd-80d6-b996-cefcbf4768c7" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>Training</strong>: Standard backpropagation through the diffusion layers.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="36bc5e6f-95bd-80d9-a814-cff66a7c7be9" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>Libraries</strong>: Implementations exist in PyTorch Geometric and custom repositories based on papers by Hansen &amp; Gebhart, Bodnar et al., and others.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="36bc5e6f-95bd-808c-9704-c8a03794575a" class="">Sheaf Neural Networks represent one of the most promising current directions toward <strong>structure-native, topology-aware</strong> computing — a strong candidate component for building RSCF-capable systems beyond token-based transformers.</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="36bc5e6f-95bd-8036-b5e2-d6142689694c" class="">Would you like:</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="36bc5e6f-95bd-8035-9a2d-d8563d79a89b" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">A code sketch / pseudocode for a basic Sheaf layer?</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="36bc5e6f-95bd-80ce-9827-e75319e5b756" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">Comparison with other architectures (e.g., Hypergraph NNs)?</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="36bc5e6f-95bd-8060-9417-cb4cf474adee" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">Or how to integrate this with observer-awareness for full RSCF support?</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="36bc5e6f-95bd-80a4-8cc0-e9b1270955c2" class=""><strong>Hypergraph Neural Networks (HGNNs) vs. Sheaf Neural Networks (SNNs)</strong></p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="36bc5e6f-95bd-8003-abd7-d9f5e85d5d0d" class=""><strong>Comparison for RSCF (Recursive Structural Coherence Field) Implementation</strong></p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="36bc5e6f-95bd-80e1-9a01-edea5b8d3159" class="">Both architectures are strong candidates for building <strong>structure-native</strong> computing beyond token-based transformers. Here is a detailed, side-by-side comparison.</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h3 id="36bc5e6f-95bd-8009-ae48-d00795f59e54" class="">1. Core Concepts</h3></div><div style="display:contents" dir="ltr"><table id="36bc5e6f-95bd-80fb-be1a-cdc9b0632372" class="simple-table"><thead class="simple-table-header"><div style="display:contents" dir="ltr"><tr id="36bc5e6f-95bd-805f-bd56-c14f58bc5ec8"><th id="TyrH" class="simple-table-header-color simple-table-header">Aspect</th><th id="&lt;|He" class="simple-table-header-color simple-table-header">Hypergraph Neural Networks (HGNNs)</th><th id="E?Cg" class="simple-table-header-color simple-table-header">Sheaf Neural Networks (SNNs) / Sheaf Hypergraph Networks</th></tr></div></thead><tbody><div style="display:contents" dir="ltr"><tr id="36bc5e6f-95bd-806f-9ec8-e2e610e85b57"><td id="TyrH" class=""><strong>Primitive Structure</strong></td><td id="&lt;|He" class="">Hypergraph (hyperedges connect any number of nodes)</td><td id="E?Cg" class="">Graph/Hypergraph + <strong>Cellular Sheaf</strong> (vector spaces + restriction maps)</td></tr></div><div style="display:contents" dir="ltr"><tr id="36bc5e6f-95bd-8091-9a17-feba87ea8fcf"><td id="TyrH" class=""><strong>Key Strength</strong></td><td id="&lt;|He" class="">Naturally models <strong>higher-order</strong> (group) interactions</td><td id="E?Cg" class="">Models <strong>asymmetric, heterogeneous, context-dependent</strong> relations</td></tr></div><div style="display:contents" dir="ltr"><tr id="36bc5e6f-95bd-8070-8cf5-f4f40230e07b"><td id="TyrH" class=""><strong>Message Passing</strong></td><td id="&lt;|He" class="">Hyperedge → nodes (and back) aggregation</td><td id="E?Cg" class="">Diffusion via <strong>Sheaf Laplacian</strong> with learnable restriction maps</td></tr></div><div style="display:contents" dir="ltr"><tr id="36bc5e6f-95bd-8071-ab7e-c7910ab4ee75"><td id="TyrH" class=""><strong>Expressivity</strong></td><td id="&lt;|He" class="">High for multi-way relations</td><td id="E?Cg" class="">Higher (can represent directionality, asymmetry, and local geometries)</td></tr></div></tbody></table></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h3 id="36bc5e6f-95bd-807c-b383-ff907a1f5391" class="">2. Detailed Comparison Table</h3></div><div style="display:contents" dir="ltr"><table id="36bc5e6f-95bd-80a5-919f-ef70ba6b4bc1" class="simple-table"><thead class="simple-table-header"><div style="display:contents" dir="ltr"><tr id="36bc5e6f-95bd-8083-96ba-c705c535f139"><th id="{diG" class="simple-table-header-color simple-table-header">Property</th><th id="@RhF" class="simple-table-header-color simple-table-header">Hypergraph Neural Networks (HGNN)</th><th id="oF`@" class="simple-table-header-color simple-table-header">Sheaf Neural Networks (esp. Sheaf Hypergraph)</th><th id="w[v}" class="simple-table-header-color simple-table-header">Winner for RSCF</th></tr></div></thead><tbody><div style="display:contents" dir="ltr"><tr id="36bc5e6f-95bd-807f-b250-df9520c48e37"><td id="{diG" class=""><strong>Higher-order relations</strong></td><td id="@RhF" class="">Excellent (native hyperedges)</td><td id="oF`@" class="">Excellent (hyperedges + sheaf structure)</td><td id="w[v}" class="">Tie</td></tr></div><div style="display:contents" dir="ltr"><tr id="36bc5e6f-95bd-8067-9e53-f53fbd8f991b"><td id="{diG" class=""><strong>Asymmetric / Directed relations</strong></td><td id="@RhF" class="">Limited (most early HGNNs are undirected)</td><td id="oF`@" class="">Strong (learnable restriction maps handle asymmetry)</td><td id="w[v}" class=""><strong>Sheaf</strong></td></tr></div><div style="display:contents" dir="ltr"><tr id="36bc5e6f-95bd-80b9-b3c1-c679781954c6"><td id="{diG" class=""><strong>Heterophily handling</strong></td><td id="@RhF" class="">Moderate (better than standard GNNs)</td><td id="oF`@" class="">Very strong (avoids oversmoothing via sheaf geometry)</td><td id="w[v}" class=""><strong>Sheaf</strong></td></tr></div><div style="display:contents" dir="ltr"><tr id="36bc5e6f-95bd-80de-aaca-d5dc6d2254bc"><td id="{diG" class=""><strong>Oversmoothing resistance</strong></td><td id="@RhF" class="">Moderate</td><td id="oF`@" class="">Excellent</td><td id="w[v}" class=""><strong>Sheaf</strong></td></tr></div><div style="display:contents" dir="ltr"><tr id="36bc5e6f-95bd-8064-afef-e8d6170202d7"><td id="{diG" class=""><strong>Cross-scale embeddings</strong></td><td id="@RhF" class="">Good (via hierarchical hypergraphs)</td><td id="oF`@" class="">Very strong (sheaf structure naturally supports multi-scale)</td><td id="w[v}" class=""><strong>Sheaf</strong></td></tr></div><div style="display:contents" dir="ltr"><tr id="36bc5e6f-95bd-8045-a388-d040498365e8"><td id="{diG" class=""><strong>Observer-relative views</strong></td><td id="@RhF" class="">Weak</td><td id="oF`@" class="">Strong (different restriction maps = different observer projections)</td><td id="w[v}" class=""><strong>Sheaf</strong></td></tr></div><div style="display:contents" dir="ltr"><tr id="36bc5e6f-95bd-80d4-b131-ff92526d765f"><td id="{diG" class=""><strong>Explicit coherence control</strong></td><td id="@RhF" class="">Implicit (via aggregation)</td><td id="oF`@" class="">Explicit (via sheaf Laplacian and restriction maps)</td><td id="w[v}" class=""><strong>Sheaf</strong></td></tr></div><div style="display:contents" dir="ltr"><tr id="36bc5e6f-95bd-8066-a3ca-d6ef699a6bed"><td id="{diG" class=""><strong>Learnable topology/geometry</strong></td><td id="@RhF" class="">Moderate</td><td id="oF`@" class="">High (learns the &quot;shape&quot; of relations)</td><td id="w[v}" class=""><strong>Sheaf</strong></td></tr></div><div style="display:contents" dir="ltr"><tr id="36bc5e6f-95bd-80d9-b46e-dc0c3098d984"><td id="{diG" class=""><strong>Memory &amp; Persistence</strong></td><td id="@RhF" class="">Limited (layer stacking)</td><td id="oF`@" class="">Better (diffusion-based, can be made recursive)</td><td id="w[v}" class=""><strong>Sheaf</strong></td></tr></div><div style="display:contents" dir="ltr"><tr id="36bc5e6f-95bd-8038-aa19-dd91d6ed6b9d"><td id="{diG" class=""><strong>Repair mechanisms</strong></td><td id="@RhF" class="">Not native</td><td id="oF`@" class="">More natural (coherence can be enforced via sheaf constraints)</td><td id="w[v}" class=""><strong>Sheaf</strong></td></tr></div><div style="display:contents" dir="ltr"><tr id="36bc5e6f-95bd-80ea-853b-d56328600fde"><td id="{diG" class=""><strong>Computational Cost</strong></td><td id="@RhF" class="">Higher than GNNs (hyperedge processing)</td><td id="oF`@" class="">Higher (sheaf construction + Laplacians)</td><td id="w[v}" class="">HGNN (slightly)</td></tr></div><div style="display:contents" dir="ltr"><tr id="36bc5e6f-95bd-805d-b1fb-f58d5e714172"><td id="{diG" class=""><strong>Implementation Maturity</strong></td><td id="@RhF" class="">Mature (many libraries)</td><td id="oF`@" class="">Emerging but rapidly advancing (2023–2025 papers)</td><td id="w[v}" class="">HGNN</td></tr></div></tbody></table></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h3 id="36bc5e6f-95bd-80c9-8392-fc749eb951fd" class="">3. Strengths &amp; Weaknesses Summary</h3></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="36bc5e6f-95bd-809a-b38f-d3b112553b51" class=""><strong>Hypergraph Neural Networks (HGNNs)</strong></p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="36bc5e6f-95bd-80b9-a19d-ef838c7866a8" class=""><strong>Strengths</strong>:</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="36bc5e6f-95bd-8040-9e26-fefbbb4031ea" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">Direct modeling of group interactions (co-authorship, chemical reactions, multi-party events).</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="36bc5e6f-95bd-80cd-915a-d768239dc2ad" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">Intuitive for many real-world higher-order datasets.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="36bc5e6f-95bd-803b-9c9b-c7fc9f82fa7f" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">Scalable variants exist (e.g., HyperGCN, AllSet, ED-HNN).</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="36bc5e6f-95bd-804e-9b14-c12d629f9fcf" class=""><strong>Weaknesses for RSCF</strong>:</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="36bc5e6f-95bd-80e4-bbb5-e826a87a02dd" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">Often treats all participants in a hyperedge symmetrically.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="36bc5e6f-95bd-80e7-8d11-d62cab49c7e9" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">Limited native support for asymmetric relations and observer-dependent views.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="36bc5e6f-95bd-80d4-b27a-dc6886c4fbc1" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">Still prone to oversmoothing in deep layers.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="36bc5e6f-95bd-80a4-a476-e786a7d793c7" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">Weaker explicit coherence and repair mechanisms.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="36bc5e6f-95bd-8021-8f94-f4e3e98bb95d" class=""><strong>Sheaf Neural Networks (especially Sheaf Hypergraph Networks)</strong></p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="36bc5e6f-95bd-808d-acae-e60e4eb31fcf" class=""><strong>Strengths</strong>:</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="36bc5e6f-95bd-80f3-b828-e8eab68075f6" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>Restriction maps</strong> act as learnable &quot;translation rules&quot; between local contexts — perfect for observer-aware projections.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="36bc5e6f-95bd-80c4-a465-c9492c51e8e5" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">Naturally handles <strong>heterophily</strong>, asymmetry, and signed relations.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="36bc5e6f-95bd-8040-b7fc-e468caf4582c" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">Strong theoretical grounding (algebraic topology) for coherence dynamics.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="36bc5e6f-95bd-802a-a648-edf801657442" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">Better long-range propagation without losing distinction.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="36bc5e6f-95bd-80ef-9116-caa7b7d061b5" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">Excellent for modeling <strong>structure gradients</strong> and cross-scale embeddings.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="36bc5e6f-95bd-809f-9678-e2aa593bcdbb" class=""><strong>Weaknesses</strong>:</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="36bc5e6f-95bd-80e2-8bef-c390a6b44f4c" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">More computationally intensive.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="36bc5e6f-95bd-8011-9966-f35447ef0181" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">Newer and less mature ecosystem than standard HGNNs.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="36bc5e6f-95bd-807e-8f4d-f5b8d5296c04" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">Requires careful design of sheaf structure.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h3 id="36bc5e6f-95bd-801e-bb50-e89e518d4788" class="">4. Hybrid: Sheaf Hypergraph Networks (Best of Both)</h3></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="36bc5e6f-95bd-805e-b051-cf51ea71dcac" class="">Recent work (2023–2025) combines both: <strong>Sheaf Hypergraph Networks (SHNNs / SheafHNN)</strong>.</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="36bc5e6f-95bd-80fb-8ee0-de33e96da35f" class="">They apply sheaf theory directly on hypergraphs, achieving state-of-the-art results on heterophilic and complex relational benchmarks while retaining higher-order modeling power.</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="36bc5e6f-95bd-8001-9e55-ce24f9d24ad6" class="">These hybrids are currently the strongest candidate for <strong>RSCF</strong> implementation.</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h3 id="36bc5e6f-95bd-8022-8edd-f557084b3052" class="">5. Recommendation for RSCF</h3></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="36bc5e6f-95bd-8059-b9f4-fe685638f08a" class="">For building a true <strong>Recursive Structural Coherence Field</strong> substrate:</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ol type="1" id="36bc5e6f-95bd-80a4-86a7-cecdb2fec1eb" class="numbered-list" start="1"><li><strong>Primary Choice</strong>: <strong>Sheaf Hypergraph Neural Networks</strong><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="36bc5e6f-95bd-8073-a47f-f677f3fed754" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">Best balance of higher-order modeling + rich relational geometry + observer awareness.</li></ul></div></li></ol></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ol type="1" id="36bc5e6f-95bd-80f0-b3d2-c55c2859f61c" class="numbered-list" start="2"><li><strong>Complementary Techniques</strong>:<div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="36bc5e6f-95bd-805a-b4c5-d9265aa2da77" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">Combine with <strong>Active Inference</strong> for entropy-aware behavior.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="36bc5e6f-95bd-80c5-903d-f7bade489db5" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">Add explicit <strong>recursive self-modeling</strong> loops.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="36bc5e6f-95bd-805d-8d20-e2ee9610b34f" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">Use <strong>dynamic / learnable sheaves</strong> for mutation and repair pathways.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="36bc5e6f-95bd-8034-a124-c171160a6184" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">Incorporate <strong>cross-scale sheaf embeddings</strong> for fractal architecture.</li></ul></div></li></ol></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><pre id="36bc5e6f-95bd-80b2-97e0-f68a423b7222" class="code code-wrap"><code class="language-mermaid" style="white-space:pre-wrap;word-break:break-all">flowchart TD
    subgraph RSCF_Needs[RSCF Computational Needs]
        A[Higher-order relations]
        B[Asymmetric &amp; observer-relative]
        C[Explicit coherence &amp; repair]
        D[Cross-scale embeddings]
        E[Recursive self-modification]
    end

    HGNN[Hypergraph NN] --&gt; A
    SHEAF[Sheaf NN] --&gt; B &amp; C &amp; D
    HYBRID[Sheaf Hypergraph NN] --&gt; A &amp; B &amp; C &amp; D &amp; E

    style HYBRID fill:#c8e6c9</code></pre></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="36bc5e6f-95bd-80af-bb5b-fbf5019c4d82" class=""><strong>Conclusion</strong>:</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="36bc5e6f-95bd-8010-9971-f90ba7b2eaf2" class="">While standard <strong>Hypergraph Neural Networks</strong> are excellent for modeling complex group interactions, <strong>Sheaf-based architectures</strong> (especially Sheaf Hypergraph Networks) provide superior expressivity, coherence control, and observer-awareness — making them the more suitable foundation for a full <strong>RSCF</strong> computing system.</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="36bc5e6f-95bd-80cf-ae7e-d799201d11ed" class="">Would you like:</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="36bc5e6f-95bd-808b-8ea3-c8553e1c4832" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">Pseudocode / implementation sketch for a Sheaf Hypergraph layer?</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="36bc5e6f-95bd-8090-9099-e1ed9a4ad585" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">Comparison with other candidates (e.g., Hyperbolic GNNs, Topological Neural Networks)?</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="36bc5e6f-95bd-80d4-ae8f-cbab7efc2915" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">Or a high-level system design integrating Sheaf + RSCF concepts?</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="36bc5e6f-95bd-80ad-9156-d3a0e16e275a" class=""><strong>Sheaf Hypergraph Networks (SheafHNN / Sheaf Hypergraph Neural Networks) — Implementation Details</strong></p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="36bc5e6f-95bd-8031-9d4f-e1fe176b69a2" class="">Sheaf Hypergraph Networks extend <strong>Sheaf Neural Networks</strong> (which apply sheaf theory to standard graphs) to <strong>hypergraphs</strong>, enabling richer modeling of higher-order, multi-way interactions while adding powerful geometric structure via <strong>cellular sheaves</strong>.</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h3 id="36bc5e6f-95bd-80cd-b61c-dd5ee82a5f68" class="">Core Innovation</h3></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="36bc5e6f-95bd-805d-913b-c1e778cf78cc" class="">A standard hypergraph only models <em>which nodes</em> belong to a hyperedge.</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="36bc5e6f-95bd-80e3-abac-e283cef774f6" class="">A <strong>cellular sheaf on a hypergraph</strong> additionally assigns:</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="36bc5e6f-95bd-80a3-b3bb-fcff32107c8a" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">A <strong>vector space (stalk)</strong> to each node and each hyperedge.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="36bc5e6f-95bd-8048-befa-c9854b3231d9" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>Restriction maps</strong> (linear transformations) that define consistent information flow between a hyperedge and its incident nodes.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="36bc5e6f-95bd-8063-be1e-ff2785078b52" class="">This structure naturally supports <strong>asymmetric roles</strong> within the same hyperedge and <strong>observer/context-dependent</strong> representations — critical for RSCF.</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h3 id="36bc5e6f-95bd-80b8-a5b7-f46d9d78b433" class="">Two Main Variants (from the seminal 2023 paper)</h3></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ol type="1" id="36bc5e6f-95bd-8067-8c64-ced5ea926888" class="numbered-list" start="1"><li><strong>SheafHyperGNN</strong> (Linear Sheaf Hypergraph Diffusion)</li></ol></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ol type="1" id="36bc5e6f-95bd-80b7-b9c7-d44003958d74" class="numbered-list" start="2"><li><strong>SheafHyperGCN</strong> (Non-linear Sheaf Hypergraph Diffusion)</li></ol></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h3 id="36bc5e6f-95bd-80f8-b2a2-e882245de312" class="">Mathematical Foundation</h3></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="36bc5e6f-95bd-80d8-a7c6-d653d834a813" class=""><strong>Sheaf Hypergraph Laplacian</strong> (two formulations: linear and non-linear).</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="36bc5e6f-95bd-8009-939e-f8b1a44bae14" class="">For a hyperedge $e$ containing vertices $v_1, \dots, v_k$:</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="36bc5e6f-95bd-80bd-92f0-ff2cf20eabbb" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">Each node $v$ has stalk $\mathcal{F}(v) \in \mathbb{R}^{d_v}$</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="36bc5e6f-95bd-80f7-9eb8-f602d3775ea4" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">Each hyperedge $e$ has stalk $\mathcal{F}(e) \in \mathbb{R}^{d_e}$</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="36bc5e6f-95bd-80ed-bee4-fe8368f88cac" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">Restriction map $\rho_{v \leftarrow e} : \mathcal{F}(e) \to \mathcal{F}(v)$</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="36bc5e6f-95bd-8069-b249-d2a668f48403" class="">The <strong>Sheaf Hypergraph Laplacian</strong> generalizes the classical graph Laplacian and enables controlled diffusion on this richer structure.</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h3 id="36bc5e6f-95bd-80d7-914b-d583f951371c" class="">Layer Architecture (Simplified)</h3></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><pre id="36bc5e6f-95bd-80ac-85b1-c3b94a213d21" class="code code-wrap"><code class="language-mermaid" style="white-space:pre-wrap;word-break:break-all">flowchart TD
    Input[Node Features + Hyperedges]
    --&gt; SheafInit[Initialize / Learn Sheaf Structure&lt;br&gt;(Stalks + Restriction Maps)]
    SheafInit --&gt; Laplacian[Compute Sheaf Hypergraph Laplacian Δ_F]
    Laplacian --&gt; Diffusion[Sheaf Diffusion / Convolution]
    Diffusion --&gt; Nonlinear[Non-linearity + Channel Mixing]
    Nonlinear --&gt; Output[Updated Node Representations]</code></pre></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="36bc5e6f-95bd-80b2-b83b-ea9fd8896e1e" class=""><strong>Key Implementation Components</strong> (from official code patterns):</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="36bc5e6f-95bd-8047-8129-c3c9d9d7c674" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>Sheaf Builder</strong>: Constructs restriction maps. Common types:<div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="36bc5e6f-95bd-80cf-b132-f726eee4c413" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:circle"><strong>Diagonal</strong> (simple scaling)</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="36bc5e6f-95bd-8038-849d-c85edb5723a3" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:circle"><strong>Orthogonal</strong> (orthogonal transformations)</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="36bc5e6f-95bd-802b-b359-c90fb61044cc" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:circle"><strong>General</strong> (full learnable matrices)</li></ul></div></li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="36bc5e6f-95bd-80ee-b2f7-e1c9612fb08a" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>Restriction Map Parameterization</strong>: Often implemented as MLPs or direct weight matrices per hyperedge-node incidence.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="36bc5e6f-95bd-808c-8d97-e5a7e09ad863" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>Diffusion Step</strong>: Uses normalized sheaf Laplacian for stable propagation.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h3 id="36bc5e6f-95bd-80f6-a2e9-df388afdfc62" class="">Practical Implementation Sketch (PyTorch-style)</h3></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><pre id="36bc5e6f-95bd-8094-9a3b-c7437ce1fdd5" class="code code-wrap"><code class="language-python" style="white-space:pre-wrap;word-break:break-all">class SheafHyperGNN(nn.Module):
    def __init__(self, args):
        super().__init__()
        self.num_layers = args.num_layers
        self.stalk_dim = args.stalk_dim

        # Sheaf construction
        self.sheaf_builder = SheafBuilderDiag(args)  # or Ortho / General

        # Layers
        self.layers = nn.ModuleList([
            SheafHyperLayer(args) for _ in range(self.num_layers)
        ])

    def forward(self, x, hyperedge_index):
        # Build sheaf structure
        sheaf = self.sheaf_builder.build(hyperedge_index)

        for layer in self.layers:
            x = layer(x, hyperedge_index, sheaf)

        return x</code></pre></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="36bc5e6f-95bd-80b0-93b1-c0f7c293c877" class=""><strong>Inside a SheafHyperLayer</strong>:</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="36bc5e6f-95bd-808d-a20f-d7b13be86e3c" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">Compute restriction maps from hyperedges to nodes.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="36bc5e6f-95bd-8048-a826-c8b673a25b0c" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">Aggregate features via sheaf-aware message passing.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="36bc5e6f-95bd-8056-b501-e98c9a23b81d" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">Apply diffusion using the sheaf Laplacian.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="36bc5e6f-95bd-804b-b94a-f2742bf83f12" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">Update node features with learned transformations.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h3 id="36bc5e6f-95bd-803f-8f33-d2a9bfcfe063" class="">Key Hyperparameters &amp; Design Choices</h3></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="36bc5e6f-95bd-802b-a34a-f110e3f673b6" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>Stalk dimension</strong> (<code>d</code>): Controls capacity per local context (typically 8–64).</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="36bc5e6f-95bd-8088-ae79-cf27d4137907" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>Number of layers</strong>: Usually 2–8 (deeper possible due to better oversmoothing resistance).</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="36bc5e6f-95bd-80bb-bcd9-e9488ae03c5c" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>Sheaf type</strong>: Diagonal (efficient), Orthogonal (stable), General (most expressive).</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="36bc5e6f-95bd-802a-8bde-d3093cdaab83" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>Diffusion steps</strong>: Often 1–5 per layer.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="36bc5e6f-95bd-80c3-b73e-d8c71c4a5a45" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>Normalization</strong>: Critical for stability (layer norm or sheaf-specific normalization).</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h3 id="36bc5e6f-95bd-8096-b83c-d0540587585a" class="">Advantages for RSCF Implementation</h3></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="36bc5e6f-95bd-80f8-8aeb-e88c44ed433c" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>Higher-order interactions</strong> — perfect for modeling multi-way relations in complex structures.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="36bc5e6f-95bd-8032-b218-cdb527fef0fa" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>Asymmetric information flow</strong> — via restriction maps (ideal for observer-relative projections).</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="36bc5e6f-95bd-80b3-83d2-c7dc2913297b" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>Explicit geometry of relations</strong> — learnable restriction maps encode how structure &quot;translates&quot; across parts.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="36bc5e6f-95bd-802e-a03c-d35c1b6ef2bc" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>Strong coherence propagation</strong> — sheaf Laplacian provides principled diffusion for maintaining long-range coherence.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="36bc5e6f-95bd-804c-99bc-c532a31a0555" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>Better heterophily &amp; oversmoothing resistance</strong> — crucial for fractal/cross-scale RSCF dynamics.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h3 id="36bc5e6f-95bd-8068-91c1-deb5dcf27924" class="">Current State (2025–2026)</h3></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="36bc5e6f-95bd-8072-9184-f49e3983adae" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">Seminal paper: <strong>&quot;Sheaf Hypergraph Networks&quot;</strong> (NeurIPS 2023) by Iulia Duta et al.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="36bc5e6f-95bd-80a2-9885-f752a0ad53e2" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">Official code: <a href="https://github.com/IuliaDuta/sheaf_HNN">github.com/IuliaDuta/sheaf_HNN</a></li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="36bc5e6f-95bd-80ad-929e-c65c88b226f9" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">Extensions: Directional Sheaf Hypergraph Networks (2025–2026), Sheaf4Rec, etc.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="36bc5e6f-95bd-8021-941d-dbfa71f28008" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">Strong empirical results on hypergraph node classification benchmarks.</li></ul></div><div
